## B. Local ID rules

Creating this mini consumes no canonical ID. One genuinely new event receives the next stable `LOCAL-<MINI_KEY>-G0001`-style key after duplicate/native-identity checking. New cards advance the local ordinal only after a complete local write and readback. Duplicates and dated addenda do not advance it. Canonical IDs are assigned only at an authorised settlement/import by repository prompt 4; no P-ID is reserved or forecast here.

Only `CHATGPT_THIS_CONVERSATION_ONLY` may append to this chat-local file; other writers require an explicit recorded handover/renewal. Previously recorded card text is preserved byte-for-byte; corrections are added as dated appendices. A frozen mini accepts no new cards. When game requests arrive, follow the sport-specific current protocol, maintain distinct receipt/started/completed/saved times, identify actual source fields and cutoff, keep unknown or unsupported contracts UNPRICED, and deliver the whole cumulative updated Markdown document.

### Decision block — research only, NOT a published forecast

The strongest *qualitative* tendencies are **first-half Over 0.5 goal** and **Under 10.5 full-match corners**. Full-match Over/Under 2.5 goals remains conflicted: Norway's four Nations League games all exceeded 2.5 goals, while three of Wales' four stayed below. These are **research leans without a calibrated, official p_card or formal rank**. The principal failures are a slow first half for the 1H Over and an early goal or pressure-driven series of set plays for the corner Under.

| Supplied contract (90-min / 1H as stated) | Historical descriptive evidence, 7 DISTINCT group matches | Analyst research disposition | p_card | Reason numerics withheld |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1H goals Over 0.5 | 5/7 unique observed fixtures had >=1 first-half goal; Norway 4/4, Wales 2/4, shared Wales–Norway counted once | QUALITATIVE LEAN OVER | UNPRICED | No validated halftime reference/process or measured lineups effect |
| 1H goals Under 0.5 | 2/7 had a goalless first half | DISFAVOURED DESCRIPTIVELY | UNPRICED | Same |
| Full match goals Over 2.5 | 4/7 unique fixtures (all four Norway fixtures) | MIXED / slight Over case, NOT STRONG | UNPRICED | Nations League opponent differences; no exact team-strength goal grid |
| Full match goals Under 2.5 | 3/7 unique fixtures (all three distinct Wales-only fixtures) | MIXED / Wales defensive game risk | UNPRICED | Same |
| Full match corners Over 10.5 | 2/7 unique fixtures exceeded 10.5 | DISFAVOURED DESCRIPTIVELY | UNPRICED | No admitted corner process, disputed NFF Den count, actual provider action unknown |
| Full match corners Under 10.5 | 5/7 unique fixtures had <=10 | QUALITATIVE LEAN UNDER | UNPRICED | Same |

**Note:** These historical frequencies are not calibrated estimates for Norway–Wales. Each seven-game union includes both sides' four 2026 Nations League matches with their 1 Oct H2H deduplicated. Different teams/venues/opponents and team selection changes make naive frequency-as-probability inappropriate. All exact O/U propositions have **no push** on their half-point thresholds.

**Formal PICKS_V2 / Rank 1 / Rank 2 / most-likely and information lists:** NOT_ISSUED / NOT_ASSESSABLE, because there is no numerically supported p_card. **Abstain:** YES for formal numeric issuance. **Potential winner (qualitative):** Norway home preference; exact 1/X/2 masses NOT_ESTIMATED. **Joint failure:** NOT_COMPUTED (distinct 1H-goal/corner processes and covariance not estimated). **Model shadow:** NOT_RUN_NO_REPOSITORY_AUTHORITY; model status NOT_VERIFIED. ### Appendix A5. Contract mechanisms and failure routes

- **1H Over 0.5:** Norway's last four A4 games all saw at least one first-half goal; four distinct Norway samples. Opening high press/transition through Ødegaard, Nusa, Bobb, Haaland is plausible but tactical mechanism is an untested inference. **Failure:** Wales' returning keeper/backline reduce creation, cautious game state leaves 0–0 at halftime.
- **1H Under 0.5:** Supported by Denmark–Wales and Wales–Denmark scoreless halves and by Wales low first-half attacking output. **Failure:** Norway's strong first-half pattern persists.
- **FT Over 2.5:** All Norway A4 games finished with 3+; Norway home conceded 2 to Portugal and Denmark; Welsh previous win against Norway also had 3. **Failure:** Welsh away attack shut out twice; they may compact defensively.
- **FT Under 2.5:** Three non-H2H Welsh fixtures had <=2 goals, so there is a credible suppression path. **Failure:** Norway's open match state, Haaland, 1+ early goals or mistakes lead to 3+.
- **Corners Under 10.5:** Both nations' shared/current games often had <=10: the unique seven A4 fixtures show 5/7 at 10 or less. **Failure:** early wide pressure, repeated blocks/crosses, trailing state or a red card drives >=11.
- **Corners Over 10.5:** Norway–Denmark (11 or 12) and Portugal–Wales (12) are examples, both involving one-sided territory or high attacking volume. **Failure:** overall corner opportunity and shot-block conversion do not reach 11; Wales historically low corner creation in A4.

### Appendix A7. Arithmetic checks and benchmark

Historical unique-event arithmetic: each side 4 completed group games, shared H2H 1 => `4 + 4 - 1 = 7` unique fixtures. Of these 7, first-half >=1 goal in 5 (Norway Denmark, Portugal home, Wales away, Portugal away; Portugal at Wales) and 0–0 first half in 2 (both Wales–Denmark fixtures). FT total >=3 in four Norway games; remaining three Wales-only fixtures are under. Total corners >=11 in Norway–Denmark and Portugal–Wales only (11 or 12 / 12); five other unique fixtures <=10. Descriptive 5/7 = 71.4%, 4/7 = 57.1%, 2/7 = 28.6%. These fractions use real outcomes but are **NOT predictive estimates**, cannot be used as frozen card probabilities, and have severe fixture dependence (teams and opponents shared). The 11-vs-12 corner conflict does not change the >10.5 classification. No coherent future joint score/corner distribution produced; complements, baseline gap, winner masses, push/void checks not applicable to a nonnumeric research card. No calculated Brier, calibration, simulation count or fit.

### Appendix A4. Opponent strength, venue, H2H and coaching

- Opponent-strength gate for the positive side: Clemson is **6-3 (4-2 ACC)** after wins over Georgia Southern, North Carolina, California, Charleston Southern, Florida State and Syracuse. Its larger losses (LSU by 41, Miami by 28) came against stronger foes than the majority of Georgia Tech's 2026 opponents; schedule class not treated as a fitted rating. Clemson is not an ordinary weak plus-handicap underdog in this exact supplied orientation.
- GT's 2 wins are Mercer (44-11) and Duke (28-25). Duke itself was 7-2 in the ACC current season snapshot, providing meaningful evidence of upside, but GT's four-game losing run contains three double-digit defeats to Boston College, Pitt and Louisville plus its loss at Virginia Tech in the five-game window. 2026 opponents and venue verified from ACC schedules; no subjective numerical opponent adjustment.
- H2H: Georgia Tech beat Clemson 24-21 at Atlanta in 2025, breaking a nine-game skid. Clemson has won eight consecutive Georgia Tech home meetings at Clemson since 2008, according to the Clemson school preview; continuity of 2025 rosters/coaches is poor, so H2H is only context. https://ramblinwreck.com/news/2026/11/2/kickoff-time-set-for-georgia-tech-clemson and https://clemsontigers.com/news/2026/01/26/clemson-announces-2026-football-schedule
- Coaching: GT fired DC Jason Semore, OL coach Allen Mogridge, edge coach Kyle Pope and WR coach Jafar Williams on Nov 9. Vinnie Sunseri took defensive play-calling duties, while WR/OL roles changed. This is a new on-field scheme/regime, not a measured +/− point effect: https://ramblinwreck.com/news/2026/11/09/fb-staff-changes . Confidence in center/variance should not be overstated.
- Stakes: Clemson 4-2 ACC retained conference positioning relevance; GT 1-5 has a losing record. Neither is a bowl/CFP elimination proof or a numeric incentive adjustment.

Georgia Tech: 20 Out; three Game-time Decisions (safety Chris Hewitt Jr., receiver Rahkeem Smith, RB Shane Marshall); injuries include TE Kevin Roche Jr., RB Malachi Hosley, CB Kelvin Hill, DE Jordan Boyd. Clemson: 13 Out, including WR Tyler Brown, RB Gideon Davidson, OL Elyjah Thurmon and Harris Sewell, DBs Donovan Starr and Corian Gipson, plus other depth losses. Gordon Sellars III listed available. The ACC official live embedded iframe https://theacc.com/sports/2025/8/28/availability-reporting-football.aspx was located but player-level lines could not be independently opened in this interface; the third-party site is a **reproduction** of official data, not independent official confirmation. Its unrelated market/pick material has been excluded from statistical inputs.

These absences materially increase uncertainty for both Clemson offense and Georgia Tech defensive protection. **No exact-scope empirical player effect is available**, so all numerical effects remain UNPRICED; no invented baseline adjustments or sham low/base/high probabilities.

### Appendix A6. Environment, tempo, total, scenarios

- Venue: Memorial Stadium Clemson, South Carolina, home grass, open air. Both teams played the previous Saturday (7 Nov); no timezone change, short travel from Georgia Tech. Conditions forecast: ~51°F / 11°C at kickoff, dry, light NNW 6 mph wind, gusts ~10 mph; source https://cfbweather.com/game/2026/georgia-tech-at-clemson-2026-11-14 . Weather effect not measured; no temperature/wind points movement invented.
- Offense/defense: Clemson current PF 22.67, PA 20.67; GT current PF 23.89, PA 32.22. Clemson's most recent 18-13 Syracuse win included a defensive touchdown (Clemson official game recap); that score is **not** evidence of 18 offense-only points. GT defense conceded 49, 34, 44 across three of its last four. Conversely Clemson had 31 combined points at Syracuse and its out RB/WR/OL undermine an assumption of 35+ offense points.
- **Low-total scenario**: Clemson defense suppresses GT's struggling offense, Clemson front and depleted offensive line result in short possessions/FGs, slower pace, ~45 or fewer combined. **High-total scenario**: GT's leaking defense permits Clemson explosive scoring, Georgia Tech plays catch-up, turnovers/short fields and non-offensive scores accelerate combined points. Both are possible, neither has an empirically anchored weight; no price assigned.
- **Clemson +15.5 failure branch**: GT's four-coach reset materially improves turnovers/field position and its offensive QB play, while Clemson's 13 unavailable players impair protection/explosive plays and GT wins by 16+. Those dependencies are not independent, but their quantitative probability is unsupported.
- NCAA key numbers 3/7, TD and FG scoring clusters, overtime and non-offensive touchdown tails must be captured in a coherent score-grid/drive process before numeric claims. Current data contain game totals only; tempo, offensive/defensive drive success, EPA, red-zone and availability-adjusted efficiencies were not verified sufficiently to compute it. A total near 47.5 cannot be priced by simply averaging PF+PA.

### Decision block — research preferences, NOT issued numeric ranking

**Primary directional read:** Alabama more likely to win outright, but Vanderbilt +14.5 has a defensible cushion given the reported move from Russell to backup QB Mack. Totals at 48.5 are ambiguous; lean Under *conditionally* if Mack starts and Alabama prioritises a lower-variance ground/control approach. No 0.65 strong-pick claim; no formal Rank-1/Rank-2 selection because exact p is unpriced.

| Vanderbilt +14.5 | **Top qualitative lean** | UNPRICED | QB uncertainty for Alabama; 14.5-point cushion, Vanderbilt home and last-week 25–23 SEC win | Alabama defensive/special-teams scores and repeated offensive explosive plays; Vanderbilt 2026 losses by 24/35/17 to SEC opponents |
| Total Over 48.5 | Balanced / secondary option | UNPRICED | Alabama 2026 37.4 PF and Vanderbilt 29.4 PA; nine-game overall combined averages 57.8 Alabama and 52.4 Vanderbilt | New Alabama QB, red-zone stalls and slower pace; opponent-adjusted strength not fitted |
| Total Under 48.5 | **Conditional second qualitative lean (low confidence)** | UNPRICED | Alabama defense 20.3 PA, Vanderbilt recent difficulties vs strong SEC teams, projected Mack start could depress Alabama efficiency | Alabama scoring 35+ against Vanderbilt defense and Vanderbilt contributing 17+, turnovers/short-field TDs |

No assumption that both qualitative leans are independent. A late Alabama offensive runaway could simultaneously defeat Vanderbilt +14.5 and Under 48.5. A home comeback/garbage-time touchdown might save the spread while pushing the Over. Rank thresholds and joint failure mass: `NOT_ASSESSABLE/NOT_COMPUTED`.

**Availability scenario descriptions (outside primary picks):** Russell starts but ankle limited; Mack starts and game-managed; Mack starts and is unexpectedly efficient; Russell enters in relief. These are qualitative sensitivities without numeric probabilities or causal effect sizes. Team home style, QB dynamics, OL absences, multiple short-field scoring events may change spread and total in the same direction. No lineup or injury effect was double-counted.

### Appendix A5. Opponent-strength/score composition and uncertainty

- Alabama 2026 aggregate: 37.44 scored/20.33 conceded, average margin +17.11 through nine games; Vanderbilt 23.00 scored/29.44 conceded, average margin −6.44 through nine. The stronger opponent context of Alabama and lower Vanderbilt defenses are real, but unadjusted margins are **not** a fitted match-specific handicap distribution.
- Naïve arithmetic only (NOT PRIMARY): averaging Alabama points-for with Vanderbilt points-against gives (37.44+29.44)/2 = 33.44; Vanderbilt points-for with Alabama points-against gives (23+20.33)/2 = 21.67. Unadjusted combined total 55.11, difference 11.77. This is not a calibrated reference or a p_card, double-counts strength/opponents, ignores Russell/Mack and may overstate the total. No formal expected score is published.

Opponent and venue differences mean this is qualitative strength evidence only, not a cross-team point adjustment. Georgia 2026: Alabama 17–20 at home, Vanderbilt 14–38 away; same opponent, different venue, strength/regime. At Mississippi State Alabama won 56–23 (Oct 3) and Vanderbilt won 25–23 (Nov 7); the time and different offensive availability prevent a fixed numerical inference.
- Alabama lost consecutive SEC games vs Georgia (17–20) and Tennessee (10–26), then beat Texas A&M (31–20) and LSU (31–13). Vanderbilt scored 14, 17, 21, 17 in consecutive Oct games against Georgia, Ole Miss, Arkansas and Kentucky; returned with 25 vs Mississippi State. Under 48.5 can lose to a Tide blowout plus garbage-time TD, even if Vanderbilt +14.5 loses concurrently.
- Discrete scoring: 3/7 touchdown/try clusters, turnovers and field goals; NCAA overtime may add possessions and push total above 48.5. A single continuous Gaussian based on nine games is unsupported; no simulated N, seed, estimated covariance or model fit claimed.

- **Competition row:** repo `research/competition_parameters/COMPETITION_PARAMETERS.csv` has `Ice Hockey/NHL`, historical development and verified store totals; scope-specific goalie and roster features for this 2026 match not frozen. - **Descriptive season arithmetic, raw unadjusted:** BUF GF=55/19=2.895, GA=68/19=3.579; DAL GF=52/18=2.889, GA=49/18=2.722. Straight symmetric mean pair (2.895+2.722)/2≈2.81 BUF goals; (2.889+3.579)/2≈3.23 DAL goals; combined≈6.04. This is *only an unaudited descriptive cross-team average*, not a dated, fitted, opponent-adjusted estimate and **not `EXPECTED_SCORE`, `REFERENCE`, `p_card` or a numeric prediction**. It ignores Heiskanen/Bichsel/Duchene, goalie regimes, PP/PK, home edge, overtime and goal dependence. Use solely as a diagnostic for why both total directions can be plausible.
- **Mandatory coherent distribution/reference not executed:** `python -m ml reference tier`, reference standings/run, pregame reference freeze, current confirmed starter pack, measured adjustment ledger, structured regulation/OT+SO object, shootout scorer convention, C21 arithmetic and AUDIT-v1 `CONTRACT_REVIEW.csv` hash-binding: **NOT_RUN**, no `PICKS_V2`, no official reference frozen, no MC/simulation, no possession/shot/xG detailed score model. **All 4 requested contract probabilities remain UNPRICED.** Do not assert 0.50, assign an arbitrary estimate, or conflate the observed 8/9 road survivals with actual forward probability.
- **Baseline trial:** `BASELINE_LEAGUE_V1` exact opponent and matching end-event/line cannot be computed from this retrieved material, because official 2026 NHL goal/OT/shootout count rules and current population goalie-dependent changes are not assembled. `BASELINE_P: NOT_ESTIMATED`, `TEAM_BASELINE_P: NOT_COVERED_WITH_CURRENT_MODEL`, `uncertainty_width: NOT_ESTIMATED`; historical league constant 6.25 (SD 2.30) is context only.
- **Width, shrinkage and OT:** NHL regulation margin and total tails differ from independent Poisson; overtime/shootout transform regulation draws to one-goal outcomes. Empty-net scenarios change +1.5 and total outcomes; all supplied lines end .5 and have no integer final-score push. A 3–3 regulation tie that proceeds to an OT/SO goal becomes 4–3 (7 total), turning Over 6.5, while 2–2 becomes 3–2 (5 total), Under 6.5. Exact operator's treatment of shootout-winning goal for total is **UNKNOWN_DEFINITION** until confirmed; maintain explicit conditional caution.
- **Low/base/high score-state sensitivity, weights NOT estimated:** LOW scoring: Dallas suppresses attempts, typical Oettinger, BUF fails on PP, 2–1/3–2 type DAL win → Stars ML, BUF +1.5, Under 6.5. BASE: balanced game with BUF road resilience, 3–2/4–3 in either direction; 4–3 pushes total Over but one-goal spread survives. HIGH: Buffalo poor PK or Dallas injured blue-line blown cover creates 5–2 / 2–5 / 5–4 routes, mixed cushion/ML but 7+ goals Over. Empty net may turn DAL 3–2 into 4–2, causing **BUF +1.5 to lose even though Under 6.5 wins**. No formal weights or probability-range endpoints without a measured supportable model. No claimed matchup-specific expected score or variance.

### A6. Rules / correlation / combined failure review

- **NHL regular season, 60-minute regulation plus 3-on-3 five-minute overtime then shootout when tied.** Exact win is full match after resolution, if `ML` uses normal NHL 2-way inclusion. Buffalo +1.5 survives Buffalo outright win, overtime/shootout one-goal loss, or Dallas regulation one-goal win; it fails on any Dallas margin ≥2 incl. empty net. Both handicap and full-match goals include OT/SO by ordinary reading but operator terms not supplied: `UNKNOWN_DEFINITION` is preserved; do not claim terms audited. Totals settle on official final goal count subject to named venue/market rules; NHL shootout adds one goal to the final tally, not all shootout attempts.
- **Dependence:** DAL ML + BUF +1.5 joint successes require *exactly Dallas one-goal victory*, including OT/SO. If BUF wins, DAL ML loses. If DAL wins by 2+, BUF +1.5 loses. Consequently, the top two are not two independently safe outcomes, and cannot both survive a DAL 4–2 late empty-net branch. No numeric conditional failure or Fréchet bounds reported because constituent p missing.
- **H2H:** DAL 4–0 BUF on 2026-10-08 (home BUF) is a source-confirmed recent direct matchup and documents the most important BUF +1.5 kill path; DAL this time at home but without three major regular skaters, and both goalies have changed context. Season rematch risk and special teams matter more than historical head-to-head win percentages.

### A7. Priorities, abstention and interpretation

**Qualitative supplied-only priority:** `1 BUF +1.5`; `2 DAL ML`; `3 Under 6.5` (weak); `4 Over 6.5` (competing alternative). **Likely straight-up winner DAL (unpriced)**, but game often tight by BUF road evidence. Main first-row kill mechanism: Dallas' PP versus Buffalo PK, Oettinger save edge, or empty net to win by 2+. Main second-row kill mechanism: DAL missing Heiskanen/Bichsel, BUF road overtime competence. Main total risk both ways: goalie efficiency/shots suppressed versus DAL PP/BUF defensive instability. Confidence for any *formal* graded selection: **NONE / NO_STRONG_PICK**, qualitative evidence moderate-low for spread, low for totals. Priorities are not issued numerical Rank-1 or Rank-2, no calibration or stake suggestion.

### A6. Competition rules, correlation, outcome structure and failure routes

- NBA regular-season regulation **4×12 min = 48 min**, 5-min overtime periods as needed; all four supplied spread/total lines cover full contest **including overtime** (BK-OT-1 frozen convention). Under/Over decimal .5 no ordinary push. A final margin ≥+5 from MEM perspective satisfies MEM -4.5; final Memphis lead 4 or fewer, tie, or any BKN win satisfies Nets +4.5. Two opposite spreads are perfect complements on a decided NBA final. Missing actual jump ball means do not assert pre-start issue cohort unconditionally.
- **State dependence:** Nets +4.5 and Under 224.5 can both occur in slow, close games; both can fail if Memphis has offensive-breakout blowout. But it is also possible for Nets +4.5 to win while total Over (e.g., 117–119) or MEM -4.5 to win with Under (e.g., 105–116). No numeric joint probabilities. A final-period backdoor cover can decide handicap while OT adds points to total. Projected model should explicitly couple turnover/pace/rebounding and late fouls.
- **Main underdog failure route:** Memphis' top-10 net rating, home team, Nets defensive vulnerabilities, second-chance boards; BKN shooting slump. **Main Under failure route:** Memphis remaining scorers run efficient offense versus Nets DRtg 115.2, BKN 145-point last outing, high scoring foul/OT tail. Main MEM −4.5 failure route: loss of Jerome's usage and Edey/Grant uncertainty lowers halfcourt creation and bench depth, tight one-possession finish. Main Over failure: absent playmaker and fatigue compress late scoring.

No odds/money advice, no fictitious Monte Carlo, no posterior or calibrated claim. Do not conflate last-five trend frequency with p.

If CHI score 100, MIN need ≥138; CHI 105 → MIN ≥133; CHI 110 → MIN ≥128; CHI 115 → MIN ≥123; CHI 120 → MIN ≥118. Under ≤237. For a MIN -15.5 cover, MIN final points must exceed CHI by at least 16; at CHI 100 MIN must score ≥116, at CHI 110 MIN ≥126, at CHI 115 MIN ≥131. CHI +15.5 succeeds on any Bulls win or MIN win by ≤15. The two spread directions are mutually exclusive and collectively exhaustive at the integer final score.

No scenario weights or measured effect, no artificial interval, no manufactured simulation or 100k Monte Carlo. Total/handicap dependence changes with leading margin, substitutions and overtime.

### A6. Basketball rules, opponent quality, combined failure and primary selection risk

- NBA 4×12-minute regulation; overtime 5 minutes until a winner; user full-game 15.5-point spread and 237.5 total settle including OT (`BK-OT-1 FROZEN_FRAMEWORK_CONVENTION`). No push at half-point thresholds. OT and late fouls can add an otherwise absent tail; blowout closing rotations can allow CHI to cover despite losing decisively.
- **Joint downside:** Chicago +15.5 and Under 237.5 can both fail when Minnesota runs away with efficient offence, Bulls' porous defence maintains enough pace to break 238 while losing by 16+. Both can also succeed when game is controlled and CHI stays within 15. A Wolves 120–100 scenario is **Under 237.5** and **MIN −15.5**, showing an Under is not a mechanical companion to Bulls +15.5. A MIN 124–115 finish is **Over 237.5** and **CHI +15.5**. These are illustrative arithmetic outcomes, not projected scores or probabilities.
- **Spread selection risk:** Minnesota 9–3, +13.4 season margin, 5–0 L5, strong defence and home profile oppose a naive Chicago cushion. Chicago's poor 2–9 overall and 1–5 away do not rule out a 15-or-fewer loss. Official questionable Powell/Claxton/Smith and short rest represent an aligned risk; therefore **CHI +15.5 is only fragile/conditional**, not rank supported or a high-confidence choice. Minnesota -15.5 is a strong alternative if Bulls' offensive/defensive minutes are thin. Historic winner isn't guaranteed from historical strong record.
- **Total risk:** Chicago 7/11 past games >237.5 while MIN 1/12 did so; the last five and opponent interaction are not one identical population. MIN defensive suppression supports Under; Chicago league-fast tempo and MIN offence vs weak CHI defence preserve Over. No certainty from small n or mechanically combining unders.
- **Selection:** SUPPLIED_ONLY candidate universe 4; one game winner qualitative MIN. Rank gating/precision tie and joint failure probability **NOT_ESTIMATED**; cannot compute p_card based on overlap/mean alone. Outcome/final exposure not used; if game had already begun, this remains after-scheduled research and must be excluded from retrospective prospective cohort.

Next, **CHI +15.5** is a *fragile cushion hypothesis*, expressly countered by opponent quality and Bulls rotation doubts; **MIN −15.5 is a live competing spread hypothesis**, not safely ruled out. Outright winning preference Minnesota. Every numeric primary remains `UNPRICED`, no simulation or unrun reference advertised. ### A6. Match process, pace and 158.5 team-score budgets

- Regulation 40 minutes, B.League court rules, overtime 5 minutes until result. Under 158.5 requires **at most 158 combined**; Over 158.5 requires **159 or more**. Numerical examples are *algebraic paths only*, **not expected scores**:
  - If Niigata scores **80**, Gifu needs **79+** to make Over, while a Gifu **78 or less** makes Under.
  - If Niigata scores **85**, Gifu needs **74+** for Over; a Gifu **73 or less** makes Under.
  - If Niigata scores **90**, Gifu needs **69+** for Over; a Gifu **68 or less** makes Under.
  - If Gifu manages **70**, Niigata needs **89+** for Over; 88 or fewer makes Under.
- Recent H2H totals were 163/163/167; last-five combined means Niigata 155.4 and Gifu 153.8; Gifu current-season combined mean 165.8. Direction of total therefore **ambiguous**, rather than a confident Over based only on H2H. Strong scorer absence and extra night can reduce efficiency; home offence and mismatch transition points may push above 159. No source-corroborated possession count, 4-factor efficiencies, shooting variance, rebound conversion / offensive rebounding rates, 5v5 unit model, or overtime contribution quantified.
- **Margin + total dependence:** Niigata can cover and Under on 85–70 (155, +15), cover and Over on 92–75 (167, +17); Gifu can cover and Over on 82–81 (163, Niigata +1); Gifu can cover and Under on 78–76 (154, Niigata +2). These are possible NBA-equivalent margin-total joint states **not simulations**, with historic examples exactly as above when coincident. A blowout can compress bench possessions late, an Over can occur with either spread winner. No unsupported joint probability/correlation stated.

### A6. Total and side process; explicit score-budget thresholds and dependence

Full-game Over185.5 iff combined points **≥186**, Under iff ≤185. **Illustrative score budgets only** (NOT simulated/expected score):

- If Brisbane scores **90**, Tasmania needs **96** to exceed 185.5; Tasmania at 95 yields total185 (Under), at 96 total186 (Over) and wins by6.
- If Brisbane scores **95**, Tasmania needs **91** for Over (total186); 90 gives total185 (Under) and Brisbane wins by5.
- If Tasmania scores **90**, Brisbane needs **96** for Over; Brisbane95 yields Under185.
- If Tasmania scores **100**, Brisbane needs **86** for Over; Brisbane85 yields Under185 despite Tasmania winning by15.

**Simple observed-score diagnostic only:** Brisbane last5 PF94.4, PA102.8; Tasmania last5 PF88.6, PA84.0. Arithmetic midpoint matchup score **Brisbane (94.4+84.0)/2 = 89.2**, Tasmania **(88.6+102.8)/2 = 95.7**, illustrative total **184.9**, illustrative Tasmania margin **6.5**, without pace/ORtg, opponent adjustment, 40-minute/OT mix, lineup or error distribution. **NOT an eligible forecast/probability/expected score**; last10 and season sample sensitivity makes this midpoint less stable. A 0.6-point difference from 185.5 is too small to confidently separate Over/Under. If one tries to use this as an empirical Over prediction, it would contradict the tiny margin and volatility; therefore preference is **extremely marginal** and no formal ranking allowed.

**Total and margin joint pathways** (possible algebra only): Tasmania 96–90 Brisbane gives total186 and Tas -1.5 cover; Tasmania 95–90 yields185 Under while Tas -1.5 covers; Brisbane 96–90 Tasmania gives total186 and Brisbane +1.5 covers; Brisbane 90–89 Tasmania gives179 Under and Brisbane+1.5 covers. No one can say these contracts are independent, and 5-min overtime can tip totals above threshold after regulation. No named 100k simulation was run, no false covariance estimate.

**Qualitative totals case:** historical H2H October3 208 OT, October21 191 (both Over); Brisbane L10 combined 197.2, Tasmania L10 184.3, L5 Tasmania 172.6, L5 Brisbane 197.2; defensively Tasmania held Sydney to71 and NZ to77. Over slightly possible if Brisbane pushes pace, faces Tasmania short-rotation bench, but Tasmania's slow defensive scores and injuries present credible Under. Neither direction qualifies high confidence.

### A8. Qualitative conclusion, principal opposing cases and next inputs

- **Primary conditional tendency:** **Suwon KT +3.5** slight, LOW confidence. Both 6–8 in current season; KT won previous October encounter by25, league-leading rebounding 37.6/g, stronger rest, while LG has high-minute back-to-back, no Carl Tamayo and (confirmed today) no Jung In-deok. **Kill route:** KT missing Oczon/Ha/Kim, LG Goodwin/Marei and 3pt shooting, KT poor away closeouts, LG can win by 4+. Doesn't automatically imply KT moneyline winner.
- **Full total:** **No robust ranked pick**. Historically LG L5 3/5 Over170.5 and KT L5 4/5; recent simple team mean ~175, but current per100/pace diagnostic ≈168 and 15 Oct H2H169, plus injury-driven creation shortfall and both teams' low closing-period output. If a single *very slight* direction is required, Over170.5 has an observed-results case, but **Under170.5 remains near equal credible**, and neither meets a confidence threshold. Total should normally be `NO_STRONG_TOTAL_SELECTION`.
- **Opposite handicap:** LG -3.5 remains close possibility with home advantage and stronger offense +4.3 ORtg, particularly if KT lacks point guard creation; not a categorical rejection.
- **Dependencies:** LG back-to-back fatigues closing, KT relies on Bass/Porter offensive rebounds; a slow close and high rebounds can pull different directions; final FT/OT effects shared across side and total.

### A6. Corners and weather mechanisms; independent source chain

- **J.League officially publishes team corners `CK` in each dated club result**, so production for both sides is **field-owner verified** (not from tip/odds). Merely summing rates gives **9.6** but does NOT account for defensive corner concession by opponent, substitution patterns, live chasing states, tempo or covariance. A 10.5 cutoff requires at least **11** total corners.

Kawasaki's 28 Oct Cup vs Tosu involved **11 own corners** despite only two match goals: direct evidence that a late pressing phase can push corners over threshold without goals; Kawasaki may need to chase aggregate in second leg. Kashiwa had 11 own corners vs Daejeon on Nov3; a high-corner tail remains.
- **Meteorology:** Japan Meteorological Agency's local 15 Nov 11:00 JST forecast for Kashiwa/Chiba https://www.data.jma.go.jp/yoho/data/jishin/met/kishoushien_Chiba_Kashiwa-shi.html warned of rain with possible heavy/thunderstorm conditions toward evening (regional hourly peak up to 30mm). A separate local hourly forecast https://www.sototenki.jp/spot/53476/1hour indicated rain near 17:00–20:00, around 11°C, wind comparatively light. ### A3. Mechanism audit and correlated failure paths

| Pregame scenario | Mechanism | Dubai −12.5 | Spartak +12.5 | Over 170.5 | Under 170.5 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Dubai balanced dominance | 6–0, 94.17 PF/G, EuroLeague-level starters; Spartak allows 80.5 | Plausible if Dubai wins by ≥13 | Failure | Could score 95+ and clear | Could fail despite Spartan low score |
| Competitive Spartak | 4–2, +3.83 mean margin, 3/4 earlier meetings within 12 points | Fails | Supported | Could go either way | Could go either way |
| Lower-tempo interior matchup | Kabengele/Shengelia/Wright/Bacon v Omić/Coleman, recent Spartak 67–51 Zadar game | Possible favorite cover even at low total | Not protected merely by low total | Risk | Supported |
| Dubai shooting spree | Dubai 39.33% seasonal 3P and 65.49% 2P in ABA league stat; large offensive tail | Supported | Main failure | Supported | Main failure |
| Fatigue/rotation | Dubai returns from EuroLeague travel 12 Nov loss, 4 games in 8 days; Walkup listed in squad but reported injured, bench uncertainty | Could suppress Dubai margin | Supported conditionally | Uncertain | Uncertain |
| Late fouling / overtime | Overtime included, close score can add ~5+ team points; late foul tries to extend possessions | Uncertain | Uncertain | Raised tail | Raised failure |

**Positive-handicap opponent-strength audit:** **Dubai are objectively strong**, undefeated +12.5 season avg margin with a published EuroLeague schedule and deep named starting five. Spartak +12.5 does not become 'safe' just because 3/4 old H2Hs were close. A 13-point favorite win is supported by their recent +14 against Zadar, +18 against SC Derby and +11 away Mega. Opponent-adjusted rating, replacement value and defeat-by-13+ distribution NOT_MEASURED; keep modest evidence grade and p UNPRICED. No independently calibrated favorite-collapse or rest effects. The total and handicap are correlated through pace, shot efficiency, rotations and blowout/late-game fouling. No fake Gaussian/Monte Carlo output.

|---|---|---|
| **Dubai −12.5** | Competent outright favorite, 6–0, +12.5 mean margin, 2P/3P efficient, deeper high-quality starting unit; can extend +14/18 margins, but 3/4 historic wins were ≤12 and Spartak 4–2 in league. **Second-choice side.** Failure: Spartak slows game, scores efficiently inside, or late backdoor. | **UNPRICED / NOT_ESTIMATED**; no ABA joint-margin distribution or AUDIT-v1 review |
| **Spartak Subotica +12.5** | **Rank-1 qualitative slight preference / LOW–MODERATE confidence**; 4–2, +3.83 season margin, prior H2H 3/4 within twelve; stronger opponent quality stressed. Failure: Dubai 100-point offense and late 13+ margin or blowout. | **UNPRICED / NOT_ESTIMATED**; incomplete opponent-strength quantitative audit |
| **Over 170.5** | Dubai games average 175.83, 3/4 H2H >=172, Dubai recent 185 and 198 totals. Could clear if Dubai offense controls. **Competing direction**. Failure: Spartak 67–51 recent defense/low pace; 40-min possessions and third-period slowdowns. | **UNPRICED / NOT_ESTIMATED**; no measured pace, OT mass or scoring covariance |
| **Under 170.5** | **Rank-2 marginal conditional preference / VERY LOW confidence** from Spartak 164.83 games, 118 last ABA total, near-line ~170.33 arithmetic diagnostic and league environment. Competing 3/4 old H2H over. Failure: Dubai high-efficiency attack and quick transition/late fouls or overtime. | **UNPRICED / NOT_ESTIMATED**; not a calibrated numerical call |

**Winner:** Dubai qualitative favorite based on 6–0 league and head-to-head 4–0; formal moneyline win p and ABA stage-specific strength adjustment NOT_ESTIMATED. A home favorite's win by up to 12 would **simultaneously** make Dubai outright winner and Spartak +12.5 winner; Dubai -12.5 requires >=13. Under and Spartak +12.5 are directionally correlated in a lower-tempo competitive script, but not equivalent: Dubai can cover at Under. No independent-bet assumption.
